                 2. A.     04.09.13 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A.  Spirit of Diversity Awards  
This evening we are pleased to recognize sixteen groups and individuals 
for our Spirit of Diversity Awards. These awards recognize those who help 
foster a climate of inclusion and diversity that creates an optimal learning 
environment for the entire school community.  
This is the first year for the Spirit of Diversity Awards, which were 
designed by the Troy Diversity Council as a way to h onor those who go 
above and beyond to ensure that every student is valued and diversity is 
celebrated within the Troy School District. We plan to continue these 
awards for years to come as a way to recognize the wonderful work that 
is done in our school community.  
We received many stellar nominations for these awards and would like to 
honor the following people. Please come forward as your name or the 
name of your organization is called. 
Spirit of Diversity Award 
Padma Kuppa, who is being recognized tonight for her continuous 
volunteer work on diversity issues in the Troy School District. Padma 
works to bridge gaps in our community and provide a voice for those who 
are not always heard. 
Rouba  Samman & Jen Hilzinger - who are being recognized tonight 
for the Schroeder World Tour, a wonderful program that exposes students 
to many other cultures through dance, music, food and fashion. 
Nadeen Brown, who is being recognized tonight for her work on behalf 
of students in the Troy School District, and especially her leadership as 
president of the Troy African American Parent Network, her compassion 
and support to minority students and their parents and her efforts to 
improve opportunities for all students through the ACT preparation 
course, Saturday tutoring program and other outreach programs.  
Tonia Romancheck, who is being recognized tonight for her efforts to 
ensure that students with special needs are included and valued in every 
part of the school community. Tonia organizes opportunities for general 
education students and moderately impaired students to socialize, become 
5
friends and realize that we are all more alike than we are different. 
TAAPN (Troy African American Parent Network), lead by Nadeen 
Brown, Aretha Bradley, Constance Dansby, Tesha Long, and Cathy 
Hodges. This group and these volunteers are being recognized for their 
work to increase cultural awareness and advocate for the academic 
success of African American students. Their many contributions to the 
Troy School Community include the Saturday Excel math series, tutoring 
programs, and support and mentoring for students and their families. The 
group’s motto is “Look for greatness in each child and you will find it.”  
Jen Hilzinger, who is being recognized tonight for her continual efforts 
to embrace and celebrate diversity in the Troy School District. Among 
other initiatives, Jen organized and developed Cultural Coffee hours where 
parents of different cultures can come together as well as Activity Days 
and Ice Cream Socials at Boulan Park Middle School, where students can 
share and celebrate as well. Jen truly embodies the spirit of inclusion in 
every aspect of her life and was one of the founding members of the 
Diversity Council. 
Troy High School Head Peer Mediators—students Nico Adamski, 
Nicholas Arsanos, Connor Hayden, Hope Keating, Justin Losey, Miranda 
Mio, Thomas O’Donovan, Alexsys Pullen, Rebecca Ress, Rachel Sacco and 
Eve Theoharis, led by teacher Gail Yax. The Head Peer Mediators are 
being recognized for their work to maintain a respectful culture at Troy 
High School that embraces diversity and empowers students to become 
part of the process. Throughout the year, this group trains more than 300 
students in areas such as cultural awareness, communications and 
problem solving. 
Bemis Cultural Coffee, led by Bemis PTO members Jeanette 
Tanafranca, Vicki Wind, Janice Girling, Mehrun-nisa Ahmad and Kendra 
Cooper, who are being recognized tonight. The Bemis Cultural Coffee 
mornings brings together parents of many different cultures to practice 
speaking English, share stories and cultural traditions and provide a 
welcoming place for new families. 
Jesse Allgeier, who is being recognized for her efforts to create and 
maintain a culture of inclusion at Troy High School. Jesse has been an 
integral part of the Troy Diversity Project for more than three years, 
working on many programs to help create a warm and inviting 
environment for all students. 
6
Honorable Mention 
Athens Theatre Company 
Sandra LewAllen, Art teacher at International Academy East 
Tom Wylie, Swim Coach at Troy High School 
Brian Nutting, Band Director at Troy High School 
Marlene Seltzer, M.D., Beaumont Hospital anti-bullying program 
Joe Thilman (Tillman), PFLAG

          2. B.      04.09.13 
2. RECOGNITION 
B.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This evening we 
are proud to recognize the accomplishments of ten  individuals who were nominated 
for the Troy School District Outstanding Teacher Award Program. 
The ten nominees represent exemplary educators at every level and were 
nominated by their peers, by parents and by former and current students, all of 
whom talked about the impact these teachers have on the lives of those around 
them. Their nomination letters described how the professionals we honor here 
tonight help students find their voices, their passion and their love for learning.  
Would you please come f orward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
First, we honor seven outstanding nominees, who have touched the lives of 
countless students and families in the Troy School District.  We are grateful for their 
dedication and passion for student success. 
Nancy Haboush, 1st Grade teacher, Leonard Elementary 
Elizabeth Mayne, Kindergarten teacher, Hamilton Elementary 
Lynn Mui, 3rd Grade teacher, Wattles Elementary 
Jessica Thudium, 4th grade teacher, Hamilton Elementary 
Karen Muzljakovich, 3rd Grade teacher, Morse Elementary 
Faith Clay, 4th Grade teacher, Bemis Elementary 
Alan Gieleghem, 3rd Grade teacher, Wattles Elementary 
Three of our district nominees were sent to Oakland Schools to represent Troy in 
the county -wide outstanding teacher award program. Due to the criteria set by 
Oakland Schools, Laurie Pickell, Kindergarten teacher at Martell Elementary School 
was nominated at the elementary level; Patrick Ko neval, Technology Teacher  
from Smith Middle School was nominated at the middle school level ; and Omar 
Hakim, English Teacher from the International Academy East was nominated at the 
high school level.  Mrs. Pickell  was also selected as the District’s overall Teacher of 
the Year and awarded a free one -year lease of a car, donated through the 
generosity of the Suburban Collection.  The three  candidates were chosen by a TEA 
selection committee to represent the District.   
At this time, I wou ld like to invite Mrs. Pickell , Mr. Koneval and Mr. Hakim  to say a 
few words.

                 2. C.    04.09.13 
2.       RECOGNITION 
C. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence Mini-Grant Recipients 
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, our 
students and our staff. The Foundation awards grants to help fund projects 
that support the curriculum in every school in the Troy S chool District. This 
year, the Foundation was able to fund  114 grants, which is a record 
number of grants in a single year. That kind of support is invaluable. 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and talents 
to raise money and s upport innovative projects that enrich student 
learning. Again, these projects take place in every school in our District and 
many would not be possible without the resources the Foundation 
provides. 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Al Robinson, President of the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize the most 
recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways they are 
enhancing the education of our Troy School District students. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Karen Rockwell will roll names of recipients/projects on 
screen as Mr. Robinson speaks.

D.  Michigan Schools & Government Credit Union Cash Grant Awards  
This evening we are pleased to recognize teachers who received cash 
grant awards from the Michigan Schools and Government Credit  Union.  
Earlier tonight , we had a short reception for our Outstanding Troy 
Teachers of the Year prior to the Board meeting.  I would like to thank the 
credit union for sponsoring the welcome reception. 
Jason Brolus, from the Michigan Schools & Government  Credit Union, will 
recognize the cash grant recipients.

attending charter schools, and schools of choice students

in  the  report  –  low,  most  likely  and  high.   She  then 
explained  how  the  projections  are  calculated  for

that projected enrollment numbers become less reliable 
the further out the projection is done for.   She provided

WHEREAS,  the  Board  of  Education  is  further 
contemplating  the  financing  of  the  proposed  Projects 
with the issuance of voter approved capital improvement 
bonds; 
WHEREAS,  the  Administration  has  prepared  an  initial 
draft  of  a  School  Improvement  Bond  Proposition, 
attached  hereto  as  Exhibit  A,  which  sets  forth  a 
preliminary  description  of  the  Projects  to  be  financed 
with the bonds; 
WHEREAS,  the  Board  is  of  the  opinion  that  if  a  bond 
proposition  is  successfully  approved  by  the  voters,  the 
bonds  issued  should  be  qualified  under  the  Michigan 
School Bond Qualification and Loan Program; and 
WHEREAS,  the  Board  is  contemplating  submitting  the 
bond  proposition  to  the  electors  at  a  future  school 
election  date  and  desires  to  authorize  the 
Superintendent  to  request  preliminary  qualification  for 
the proposed bonds from the Department of Treasury as 
required  under  the  Michigan  School  Bond  Qualification 
and Loan Program. 
NOW,  THEREFORE,  BE  IT  RESOLVED  BY  THE  BOARD  OF 
EDUCATION OF THE TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT, OAKLAND 
COUNTY, MICHIGAN, THAT: 
1. The Superintendent and/or her designee is authorized 
to  select  and  work  with  the  architect,  bond  counsel, 
construction manager, technology designer and financial 
advisor  and  take  the  necessary  action  required  to 
prepare  an  Application  for  Preliminary  Qualification  of 
Bonds for the submission to the Department of Treasury 
and  to  attend  a  Preliminary  Qualification  meeting  with 
the Department of Treasury to review the Application for 
Preliminary Qualification of Bonds. 
2.  A  resolution  approving  the  submission  of  a  final  draft 
of  the  Application  for  Preliminary Qualification  of  Bonds 
which will include the final Bond Proposition as approved 
by  the  Department  of  Treasury  will  be  presented  to  the 
Board of Education at a future meeting.  
3. All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they 
conflict with the provisions of this resolution are hereby 
rescinded.

Youth Assistance event called “Dollars for Education” and

    7. A.   04.09.13 
7. CURRICULUM 
A. REPORT:  Elementary and Middle School Mathematics Materials    
                        Adoption with Select High School Courses 
Throughout fall and winter of the 20 12-2013 school year, elementary teacher 
representatives began the process of clarifying the knowledge and skills that 
students need to be college and career ready in mathematics.  These t eachers 
deepened their knowledge of the Common Core State Standards and the 
Standards for Mathematica l practice so that they could critically examine  
materials that would support instruction at the  level required by the new 
standards. Also during this  school year, our Middle School teachers had the 
opportunity to participate in a trial of Big Ideas Math which is a  complete middle 
school math program developed with the Common Core Standards for 
Mathematical Content and Standards for Mathematical Practice as its foundation. 
Additionally, several high school mathematics courses needed updated materials 
and these were reviewed by our instructional staff as well.  Teacher 
representatives worked diligently and this evening we will be presenting these 
recommendations to the Board.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given by Kris 
Karbon, Troy School District Math Coordinator

Troy Mathematics  
in the Common Core Era

181
Four Domains for Review 
•Common Core Alignment & Design 
•Assessment & Questioning 
•Equity & Differentiation 
•Technology & Resources 
182
Common Core Alignment & 
Design 
Focused – Fewer standards in each grade level but higher 
expectations in terms of depth of knowledge.  Students 
need time to master concepts before moving on. 
Balanced – Common Core demands that students have 
procedural fluency (“how to do it”), conceptual 
understanding (“why it works”) and strategic competence 
(“when to do it”). 
Coherent – Learning follows a trajectory that allows 
students to build their understanding while making 
connections between concepts 
183
Assessment & Questioning 
Students can explain and apply mathematical concepts and carry out 
mathematical procedures with precision and fluency. 
Students can frame and solve a range of complex problems in pure 
and applied mathematics. 
Students can clearly and precisely construct viable arguments to 
support their own reasoning and to critique the reasoning of others. 
Students can analyze complex, real-world scenarios and can use 
mathematical models to interpret and solve problems.

 7. B.   04.09.13 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.     REPORT:  District MEAP Results  
The Troy School District has a comprehensive assessment 
system that includes district and school developed common 
assessments as well as other student achievement measures.  
One part of our district’s assessment program is the Michigan 
Educational Assessment Program—also called “the MEAP.” 
The MEAP assesses students in grades 3  through 9 in math, 
reading, science, and social studies.  Results of the MEAP are 
used to monitor continuous improvement.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report will be given by Anne 
Mull, Director of Instruction and Assessment.

 10. A.       04.09.13 
10. OTHER 
         A.     First Reading:  Board Policy G-1007, Layoff and Recall of  
           Teachers and Administrators 
 Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to review the new  
Board Policy G-1007, Layoff and Recal l of Teachers and Administrators . 
The policy has  been reviewed by the Board of Education’s attorney to 
make sure it complies with State law, the Open Meetings Act, and the 
Revised School Code. 
 The first reading of the new Board Policy  is presented at th is evening’s 
meeting.   
G-1007 Layoff and Recall of Teachers and Administrators 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Ask a Board member to read this policy.  
226
   GENERAL SECTION      G-1000 
G-1007 LAYOFF AND RECALL OF TEACHERS AND ADMINISTRATORS 
Teacher and administrator layoff and recall decisions shall be made based on the goal of retaining 
effective teachers and administrators.  Therefore, when conducting  a staffing or program reduction, 
recalling teachers and administrators from a staffing or program reduction, making any other personnel 
decision resulting in the elimination of a position, hiring after a staffing or program reduction, or making 
any other personnel determination related to such decisions, the Superintendent and administrative 
staff shall ensure that all such decisions are based on the retention of effective teachers and 
administrators. 
All criteria used when making decisions relating to per sonnel reduction of teachers and administrators 
shall be construed and applied in a manner consistent with section 1248 of the Michigan Revised School 
Code, as it may be amended from time to time. 
Personnel decisions involving teachers and administrators w ithin the scope of this policy shall not use 
length of service or tenure status as the primary or determining factors, and these factors shall apply 
only in the circumstances stated provided under the law, within this policy and its implementing 
regulations. 
For purposes of this policy, “teachers” include individuals whose employment is regulated by the Tenure 
Act, individuals with teaching certificates (as defined by the Teacher Certification Code) who are assigned 
to positions within the District for whic h the Michigan Department of Education (MDE) requires a 
teaching certificate.  Individuals who may possess a teaching certificate, but are assigned to a position 
for which such a certificate is not required, are not subject to this policy or its implementi ng regulations 
(e.g., school social workers, school psychologists, school nurses, occupational therapists, etc.).   
For purposes of this policy, “administrators” include administrators with instructional responsibilities 
who have acquired tenure within the District or who are serving a probationary period under the Tenure 
Act. 
The development and content of policies and procedures relating to decisions concerning the layoff and 
recall of teachers and administrators shall not be the subject of any terms and conditions within a 
collective bargaining agreement between the District and employees who are covered by this policy. 
The Superintendent or designee shall develop administrative guidelines/procedures/rules/regulations to 
support this policy. 
Source:  Section 380.1248 of the Revised School Code; Section 423.215(3)(k). 
Adopted: [Date TBD]

 10. B.       04.09.13 
10. OTHER 
         B.     First Reading:  Board Policy G-1008, Discipline, Demotion, or  
           Dismissal of Teachers and Administrators 
 Members of the Board Policy Committee have met to review the new Board  
 Policy G-1008, Discipline, Demotion, or Dismissal of Teachers and  
 Administrators. The policy has been reviewed by the Board of Education’s  
 attorney to make sure it complies with State law, the Open Meetings Act, and 
 the Revised School Code. 
 The first reading of the new Board Policy  is presented at this evening’s 
meeting.   
G-1008 Discipline, Demotion, or Dismissal of Teachers and 
Administrators 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Ask a Board member to read this policy.  
228
   GENERAL SECTION      G-1000 
G-1008 DISCIPLINE, DEMOTION, OR DISMISSAL OF TEACHERS AND ADMINISTRATORS 
Teachers and administrators whose employment is regulated by the provisions of the 
Michigan Teachers’ Tenure Act, MCL §§38.71 through 38.191, inclus ive (“Tenure Act”), 
shall be disciplined, demoted or dismissed only for a reason that is not arbitrary or 
capricious.  This Board shall not adopt, implement, or maintain a policy or standard for 
discipline or dismissal of such employees that is different f rom the arbitrary or 
capricious standard within Section 1, Article IV of the Tenure Act, MCL §38.101. 
The Superintendent or designee shall ensure that decisions regarding the discipline, 
demotion, or dismissal of public employees whose employment is regula ted by the 
Tenure Act are consistent with this policy. 
The development, content, standards, procedures, adoption, and implementation of 
policies and procedures relating to the discipline, demotion, or dismissal of teachers 
and administrators whose employme nt is regulated by the Michigan Teachers Tenure 
Act, as well as administrative decisions concerning the discipline, demotion, or 
dismissal of such employees, shall not be the subject of any terms or conditions within 
a collective bargaining agreement betwe en the District and a collective bargaining 
representative of teachers or administrators.  
The Superintendent or designee may develop and adopt administrative 
guidelines/procedures/rules/regulations that detail the standards or procedures for the 
discipline, demotion, and/or dismissal of the employees subject to this policy. 
 Source: MCL §§38.71-191; MCL §423.215(3)(m). 
Adopted: [Date TBD]
